<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the interior regularity assertion of the planar Mumford–Shah conjecture for reduced absolute minimizers with bounded fidelity data. Every interior point of the closed discontinuity set has a neighborhood consisting of a $`C^{1,\alpha}`$ arc, an arc ending at that point, or three such arcs meeting at 120 degrees. Only finitely many global connected components meet any relatively compact open set.

- **claim-interior-models-and-component-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $(u,K)$ be a reduced absolute minimizer of <a href="#eq:original-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-energy">[eq:original-energy]</a> in a bounded Lipschitz domain $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^2$, with $g\in L^\infty(\Omega)$. Every $x\in K$ has an interior neighborhood in which $K$ has one of the following forms, with $C^{1,\alpha}$ arcs for some $\alpha>0$:* 1. *a single embedded arc with $x$ in its interior;* 2. *a single embedded arc ending at $x$;* 3. *three embedded arcs meeting only at $x$, with pairwise angles $120$ degrees.* *Moreover, for every open $U\Subset\Omega$, $$\label{eq:component-finiteness} \#\{C\in\pi_0(K):C\cap U\ne\varnothing\}<\infty,$$ where $\pi_0(K)$ denotes the set of global connected components of $K$.
  - Explanation: Every interior crack point has a regular arc, endpoint, or equal-angle triple-junction neighborhood. Each relatively compact open region meets only finitely many global connected components of the crack set.
- **claim-rectifiable-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-initial-reduction)): Let $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^2$ be bounded and open, let $g\in L^\infty(\Omega)$, and let $(u,K)$ be an admissible absolute minimizer for $\mathcal F_g$ as defined in Section <a href="#sec:problem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:problem">1.1</a>. In particular, $K$ is relatively closed, $\mathcal H^1(K)<\infty$, and $u\in L^2(\Omega)\cap W^{1,2}(\Omega\setminus K)$; no rectifiability of $K$ is assumed. Then $$u\in L^\infty_{\rm loc}(\Omega)\cap SBV_{\rm loc}(\Omega), \qquad \mathcal H^1(K\setminus J_u)=0.$$ The function satisfies $\Delta u=u-g$ on $\Omega\setminus K$ and has there a $C^{1,\alpha}_{\rm loc}$ representative for every $\alpha\in(0,1)$. If the pair is reduced, then $$K=\operatorname{spt}_{\Omega}(\mathcal H^1\!\llcorner J_u) =\overline{J_u}^{\,\Omega}. \label{eq:jump-support}$$ Consequently, in the reduced case, on every smooth open set $W\Subset\Omega$, the restricted pair is a reduced rectifiable absolute minimizer with bounded function.
  - Explanation: The original finite-length formulation already forces local boundedness and special bounded variation. The crack carries no excess length beyond the jump set; reducedness identifies the closed crack with the jump-length support and permits restriction to bounded rectifiable minimizing pairs.
- **claim-smooth-data-dirichlet-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dirichlet-input)): Let $D\subset\mathbb R^2$ be a disk, $g\in L^\infty(D)$, and $w\in C^1_c(\mathbb R^2)$. The problem <a href="#eq:weak-dirichlet-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-dirichlet-energy">[eq:weak-dirichlet-energy]</a> has a bounded minimizer $z$, and $$\mathcal H^1\bigl((\overline{J_z}\setminus J_z) \cap\overline D\bigr)=0. \label{eq:dirichlet-essential-closure}$$ In particular, $C:=\overline{J_z}\cap\overline D$ is compact and has finite length, and $z$ belongs to $W^{1,2}_{\rm loc}$ on the complement of $\overline{J_z}$. On each relatively open portion of $\partial D$ disjoint from $C$, its interior and exterior Sobolev traces agree almost everywhere.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary disk problem admits a bounded weak minimizer. Closing its jump set adds no length, including on the disk boundary, and jump-free boundary portions have matching Sobolev traces.
- **claim-planar-compactness-and-regularity-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-item-epsilon)): The following facts hold.* 1. * Let $(u,K)$ be a reduced, rectifiable absolute minimizer of the fidelity functional in an open set $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^2$, with bounded fidelity datum and locally bounded $u$. For each $x\in K$ and each sequence $r_j\downarrow0$, a subsequence of $$K_j=\frac{K-x}{r_j},\qquad u_j(y)=\frac{u(x+r_jy)}{\sqrt{r_j}}$$ on the expanding domains $(\Omega-x)/r_j$ has a global generalized limit $(v,H,\{p_{kl}\})$. The sets converge locally in the bilateral Hausdorff sense. The set $H$ is closed, rectifiable, locally of finite length, and is the support of $\mathcal H^1\!\llcorner H$; moreover $0\in H$. For every bounded open $D\Subset\mathbb R^2$, $v\in W^{1,2}(D\setminus H)$.* 2. * If a global generalized minimizer has connected complement $D=\mathbb R^2\setminus H$, then $(v,H)$ is an absolute minimizer of the homogeneous energy $E_0$ under every compactly supported change. In particular, changes whose support meets $H$ are allowed.* 3. * Every global generalized minimizer has a reduced, closed, rectifiable set $H$ of locally finite length, and $v\in W^{1,2}(D\setminus H)$ for every bounded open $D\Subset\mathbb R^2$. There are positive dimensional constants $c,C$ such that $$c r\leq\mathcal H^1(H\cap B_r(y))\leq C r \quad(y\in H,\ r>0),$$ $$\int_{B_R\setminus H}\|\nabla v\|^2\,dx\leq 2\pi R \quad(R>0).$$ Almost every point of $H$ with respect to $\mathcal H^1$ has a neighborhood in which the entire set is a single $C^1$ arc.* 4. * A global generalized minimizer has at most one unbounded connected component of its closed set. If its closed set disconnects the plane, that set is a line or three half-lines meeting at $120$ degrees. If all but at most one connected component of its closed set lie in one compact set, the set is empty, a line, three half-lines meeting at $120$ degrees, or a half-line.* 5. *There are $\alpha\in(0,1)$ and $\varepsilon>0$ with the following property, with the constants chosen for the fixed bounds on the fidelity datum. Suppose that a reduced absolute minimizer is defined in a neighborhood of $\overline{B_{2s}(x)}$, where $s\leq1$. If its closed set in $B_{2s}(x)$ has bilateral Hausdorff distance less than $\varepsilon s$ from a diameter, a radius, or three radii meeting at $120$ degrees, then its entire intersection with $B_s(x)$ is $C^{1,\alpha}$-diffeomorphic to that model. Any three arcs meeting in the smaller ball meet at $120$ degrees. The endpoint or junction in this conclusion need not be the original center $x$.
  - Explanation: The recalled planar theory supplies generalized blow-up limits, absolute comparison when the limiting complement is connected, density and energy estimates, conditional global classifications, and transfer of sufficiently close models to regular local geometry.
- **claim-isolation-of-bounded-components** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-isolation)): Let $H\subset\mathbb R^2$ be closed, locally of finite length, and equal to the support of $\mathcal H^1\!\llcorner H$. Every nonempty bounded connected component of $H$ is contained in a nonempty compact subset $A\subset H$ such that, with $B=H\setminus A$, $$B\text{ is closed},\qquad \operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0,\qquad 0<\mathcal H^1(A)<\infty.$$ Here the distance to the empty set is interpreted as infinity. If $\mathbb R^2\setminus H$ is connected, $\mathbb R^2\setminus B$ is connected.
  - Explanation: A bounded component can be enlarged to a positive-length compact piece separated from the closed remainder. The enlarged piece need not itself be connected. Removing it preserves connectedness of the complement when that complement was connected.
- **claim-exclusion-of-separated-compact-pieces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-compact-piece)): Let $H\subset\mathbb R^2$ be closed, rectifiable, and locally of finite $\mathcal H^1$ measure, and assume $D=\mathbb R^2\setminus H$ is connected. Let $(v,H)$ be an absolute minimizer of $E_0$ under compactly supported comparisons, with $$v\in W^{1,2}(B_R\setminus H)\quad(R>0),\qquad \int_{B_R}\|\nabla v\|^2\leq C(1+R)\quad(R\geq1).$$ There is no compact set $A\subset H$ satisfying all of the following:* 1. *$\mathcal H^1(A)>0$;* 2. *$B=H\setminus A$ is closed and $\operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0$, with the distance interpreted as $+\infty$ if $B=\varnothing$;* 3. *$A$ contains an open subarc of $H$ that is an embedded $C^1$ arc.* *In the third condition, an open subarc means that some open neighborhood meets $H$ in just that arc.
  - Explanation: Connected-complement absolute minimizers with the stated Sobolev control and energy growth cannot contain a separated positive-length compact piece possessing a regular arc neighborhood.
- **claim-compact-jump-projection-and-decay** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-projection)): Let $A\subset\mathbb R^2$ be compact and have zero area. Suppose $h\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^2\setminus A)$ has compact support, and write $e=\nabla h$ for its gradient density. Let $$Z=\overline{\{\nabla\zeta:\zeta\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2)\}} ^{\,L^2(\mathbb R^2;\mathbb R^2)},$$ and let $Q$ be the orthogonal projection onto $Z$. There is a unique $\phi\in W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^2)$ satisfying $\nabla\phi=Qe$ and tending to zero at infinity. Set $$F=h-\phi\quad\hbox{on }\mathbb R^2\setminus A, \qquad p=e-\nabla\phi\quad\hbox{on }\mathbb R^2.$$ Then $p\in L^2(\mathbb R^2;\mathbb R^2)$, $F\in W^{1,2}(B_R\setminus A)$ for every $R>0$, and $\nabla F=p$ off $A$. Distributionally on the whole plane, $$\operatorname{div}p=0,\qquad \operatorname{supp}(\operatorname{curl}p)\subset A,\qquad \Delta p=\nabla^\perp\operatorname{curl}p. \label{eq:compact-field-equations}$$ Outside a sufficiently large disk, $F$ and $p$ are smooth, and $$F(x)=O(\|x\|^{-1}),\qquad \|\nabla^k F(x)\|=O_k(\|x\|^{-1-k})\quad(k\geq1). \label{eq:compact-field-decay}$$ In particular $p(x)=O(\|x\|^{-2})$ there.
  - Explanation: Orthogonal projection produces a uniquely normalized whole-plane potential and a finite-energy divergence-free field. The curl of that field is supported on the compact set; the field itself is not asserted to have compact support. Exterior harmonicity supplies decay.
- **claim-finite-translation-energy-inequality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-translated-comparison)): Let $H=A\cup B\subset\mathbb R^2$ be closed, rectifiable, and locally of finite $\mathcal H^1$ measure, where $A$ is compact, $B$ is closed, and $\operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0$. Let $(v,H)$ be an absolute minimizer of $E_0$ under compactly supported comparisons, with $$v\in W^{1,2}(B_R\setminus H)\quad(R>0),\qquad \int_{B_R}\|\nabla v\|^2\leq C(1+R)\quad(R\geq1).$$ Suppose $v=f+F$ off $H$, where $f\in W^{1,2}(B_R\setminus B)$ for every $R>0$, and $(F,p)$ is obtained from a compactly supported $h\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^2\setminus A)$ by Lemma <a href="#lem:compact-projection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:compact-projection">7</a>. Put $G=\nabla v-p$, and assume $$G=\nabla f\text{ off }B,\qquad \int_{B_R}\|G\|^2\leq C'(1+R)\quad(R\geq1).$$ For any compactly supported $h'\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^2\setminus A)$, let $(P,q)$ be its counterpart of $(F,p)$ in Lemma <a href="#lem:compact-projection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:compact-projection">7</a>. Choose $\delta>0$ so that $A_t\cap B=\varnothing$ whenever $\|t\|<\delta$. Define $$I_p(t)=\int_{\mathbb R^2}G\cdot p_t, \qquad I_q(t)=\int_{\mathbb R^2}G\cdot q_t.$$ Then these integrals converge absolutely, and for every $\|t\|<\delta$ and $\sigma\in\mathbb R$, $$0\leq 2\bigl(I_p(t)-I_p(0)\bigr) +2\sigma\bigl(I_q(t)+\langle p,q\rangle_{L^2}\bigr) +\sigma^2\\|q\\|_{L^2}^2. \label{eq:translated-energy}$$
  - Explanation: A distant cutoff turns translation and an independent jump perturbation into compactly supported competitors. Length and translated self-energy cancel, yielding an absolutely convergent interaction inequality without subtracting infinite whole-plane energies.
- **claim-harmonic-interaction-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-harmonic-interaction)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#lem:translated-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:translated-comparison">8</a>, suppose in addition that $f$ is harmonic on $O=\mathbb R^2\setminus B$. After decreasing $\delta$ if necessary, $I_p$ is harmonic on $B_\delta(0)$ as a function of the translation parameter. Consequently $$I_p(t)=I_p(0),\qquad I_q(t)=-\langle p,q\rangle_{L^2} \quad(\|t\|<\delta) \label{eq:constant-interactions}$$ for every compactly supported $h'$ allowed in Lemma <a href="#lem:translated-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:translated-comparison">8</a>.
  - Explanation: Harmonicity of the background makes the translated interaction harmonic. Minimality gives a minimum at the origin, forcing constancy; allowing both signs of every jump perturbation then forces its linear interaction coefficient to vanish.
- **claim-global-generalized-limit-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-classification)): The closed set of every global generalized minimizer arising from absolute minimizers is empty, a line, a $120$-degree triod of half-lines, or a half-line.
  - Explanation: Excluding bounded components closes the remaining classification gap for generalized limits arising from absolute minimizers. Their closed sets are empty or one of the stated line, ray, and triod models.
- **claim-local-weak-gradient-integrability** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-weak-l4)): Let $(u,K)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Let $\nabla u$ denote the approximate gradient furnished by Proposition <a href="#prop:initial-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:initial-reduction">2</a>; it agrees almost everywhere on $\Omega\setminus K$ with the gradient in <a href="#eq:original-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-energy">[eq:original-energy]</a>. For every open $U\Subset\Omega$ there is a constant $C_U<\infty$, depending on $U$ and the minimizer, such that $$\label{eq:weak-l4} \mathcal L^2\bigl(\{x\in U\setminus K:\|\nabla u(x)\|>t\}\bigr) \le C_Ut^{-4}\qquad\text{for every }t>0,$$ where $\mathcal L^2$ denotes planar Lebesgue measure. In particular, $\nabla u\in L^{4,\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)$ and $\nabla u\in L^p_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)$ for every $0<p<4$.
  - Explanation: Absence of irregular crack points implies the local weak endpoint gradient estimate. The constant depends on the interior region and the minimizer; strong local integrability follows only for the stated smaller exponents.

      "id": "claim-interior-models-and-component-finiteness",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "Every $x\\in K$ has an interior neighborhood in which $K$ has one of the following forms, with $C^{1,\\alpha}$ arcs for some $\\alpha>0$:*\n\n1.  *a single embedded arc with $x$ in its interior;*\n\n2.  *a single embedded arc ending at $x$;*\n\n3.  *three embedded arcs meeting only at $x$, with pairwise angles $120$ degrees.",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "Moreover, for every open $U\\Subset\\Omega$, $$\\label{eq:component-finiteness}\n \\#\\{C\\in\\pi_0(K):C\\cap U\\ne\\varnothing\\}<\\infty,$$ where $\\pi_0(K)$ denotes the set of global connected components of $K$.",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-thm-main"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-initial-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Then $$u\\in L^\\infty_{\\rm loc}(\\Omega)\\cap SBV_{\\rm loc}(\\Omega),\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal H^1(K\\setminus J_u)=0.$$ The function satisfies $\\Delta u=u-g$ on $\\Omega\\setminus K$ and has there a $C^{1,\\alpha}_{\\rm loc}$ representative for every $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$. If the pair is reduced, then $$K=\\operatorname{spt}_{\\Omega}(\\mathcal H^1\\!\\llcorner J_u)\n   =\\overline{J_u}^{\\,\\Omega}.\n \\label{eq:jump-support}$$ Consequently, in the reduced case, on every smooth open set $W\\Subset\\Omega$, the restricted pair is a reduced rectifiable absolute minimizer with bounded function.",
        "source_id": "PM-366.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "The problem <a href=\"#eq:weak-dirichlet-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weak-dirichlet-energy\">[eq:weak-dirichlet-energy]</a> has a bounded minimizer $z$, and $$\\mathcal H^1\\bigl((\\overline{J_z}\\setminus J_z)\n                  \\cap\\overline D\\bigr)=0.\n \\label{eq:dirichlet-essential-closure}$$ In particular, $C:=\\overline{J_z}\\cap\\overline D$ is compact and has finite length, and $z$ belongs to $W^{1,2}_{\\rm loc}$ on the complement of $\\overline{J_z}$.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-item-epsilon",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:planar-facts\" label=\"prop:planar-facts\"></span> The following facts hold.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"item:blowup\" label=\"item:blowup\"></span> Let $(u,K)$ be a reduced, rectifiable absolute minimizer of the fidelity functional in an open set $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^2$, with bounded fidelity datum and locally bounded $u$. For each $x\\in K$ and each sequence $r_j\\downarrow0$, a subsequence of $$K_j=\\frac{K-x}{r_j},\\qquad\n     u_j(y)=\\frac{u(x+r_jy)}{\\sqrt{r_j}}$$ on the expanding domains $(\\Omega-x)/r_j$ has a global generalized limit $(v,H,\\{p_{kl}\\})$. The sets converge locally in the bilateral Hausdorff sense. The set $H$ is closed, rectifiable, locally of finite length, and is the support of $\\mathcal H^1\\!\\llcorner H$; moreover $0\\in H$. For every bounded open $D\\Subset\\mathbb R^2$, $v\\in W^{1,2}(D\\setminus H)$.*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"item:connected-absolute\" label=\"item:connected-absolute\"></span> If a global generalized minimizer has connected complement $D=\\mathbb R^2\\setminus H$, then $(v,H)$ is an absolute minimizer of the homogeneous energy $E_0$ under every compactly supported change. In particular, changes whose support meets $H$ are allowed.*\n\n3.  *<span id=\"item:global-estimates\" label=\"item:global-estimates\"></span> Every global generalized minimizer has a reduced, closed, rectifiable set $H$ of locally finite length, and $v\\in W^{1,2}(D\\setminus H)$ for every bounded open $D\\Subset\\mathbb R^2$. There are positive dimensional constants $c,C$ such that $$c r\\leq\\mathcal H^1(H\\cap B_r(y))\\leq C r\n     \\quad(y\\in H,\\ r>0),$$ $$\\int_{B_R\\setminus H}|\\nabla v|^2\\,dx\\leq 2\\pi R\n     \\quad(R>0).$$ Almost every point of $H$ with respect to $\\mathcal H^1$ has a neighborhood in which the entire set is a single $C^1$ arc.*\n\n4.  *<span id=\"item:classification\" label=\"item:classification\"></span> A global generalized minimizer has at most one unbounded connected component of its closed set. If its closed set disconnects the plane, that set is a line or three half-lines meeting at $120$ degrees. If all but at most one connected component of its closed set lie in one compact set, the set is empty, a line, three half-lines meeting at $120$ degrees, or a half-line.*\n\n5.  *There are $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$ and $\\varepsilon>0$ with the following property, with the constants chosen for the fixed bounds on the fidelity datum. Suppose that a reduced absolute minimizer is defined in a neighborhood of $\\overline{B_{2s}(x)}$, where $s\\leq1$. If its closed set in $B_{2s}(x)$ has bilateral Hausdorff distance less than $\\varepsilon s$ from a diameter, a radius, or three radii meeting at $120$ degrees, then its entire intersection with $B_s(x)$ is $C^{1,\\alpha}$-diffeomorphic to that model. Any three arcs meeting in the smaller ball meet at $120$ degrees. The endpoint or junction in this conclusion need not be the original center $x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-366.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-item-epsilon",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-compact-piece",
        "exact_quote": "There is no compact set $A\\subset H$ satisfying all of the following:*\n\n1.  *$\\mathcal H^1(A)>0$;*\n\n2.  *$B=H\\setminus A$ is closed and $\\operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0$, with the distance interpreted as $+\\infty$ if $B=\\varnothing$;*\n\n3.  *$A$ contains an open subarc of $H$ that is an embedded $C^1$ arc.*\n\n*In the third condition, an open subarc means that some open neighborhood meets $H$ in just that arc.",
        "source_id": "PM-366.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-compact-piece",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be closed, rectifiable, and locally of finite $\\mathcal H^1$ measure, and assume $D=\\mathbb R^2\\setminus H$ is connected. Let $(v,H)$ be an absolute minimizer of $E_0$ under compactly supported comparisons, with $$v\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus H)\\quad(R>0),\\qquad\n \\int_{B_R}|\\nabla v|^2\\leq C(1+R)\\quad(R\\geq1).$$ There is no compact set $A\\subset H$ satisfying all of the following:*\n\n1.  *$\\mathcal H^1(A)>0$;*\n\n2.  *$B=H\\setminus A$ is closed and $\\operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0$, with the distance interpreted as $+\\infty$ if $B=\\varnothing$;*\n\n3.  *$A$ contains an open subarc of $H$ that is an embedded $C^1$ arc.*\n\n*In the third condition, an open subarc means that some open neighborhood meets $H$ in just that arc.",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $H\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be closed, rectifiable, and locally of finite $\\mathcal H^1$ measure, and assume $D=\\mathbb R^2\\setminus H$ is connected.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-projection",
        "exact_quote": "There is a unique $\\phi\\in W^{1,2}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbb R^2)$ satisfying $\\nabla\\phi=Qe$ and tending to zero at infinity. Set $$F=h-\\phi\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathbb R^2\\setminus A,\n \\qquad p=e-\\nabla\\phi\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathbb R^2.$$ Then $p\\in L^2(\\mathbb R^2;\\mathbb R^2)$, $F\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus A)$ for every $R>0$, and $\\nabla F=p$ off $A$. Distributionally on the whole plane, $$\\operatorname{div}p=0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{supp}(\\operatorname{curl}p)\\subset A,\\qquad\n \\Delta p=\\nabla^\\perp\\operatorname{curl}p.\n \\label{eq:compact-field-equations}$$ Outside a sufficiently large disk, $F$ and $p$ are smooth, and $$F(x)=O(|x|^{-1}),\\qquad\n |\\nabla^k F(x)|=O_k(|x|^{-1-k})\\quad(k\\geq1).\n \\label{eq:compact-field-decay}$$ In particular $p(x)=O(|x|^{-2})$ there.",
        "source_id": "PM-366.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-projection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be compact and have zero area. There is a unique $\\phi\\in W^{1,2}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbb R^2)$ satisfying $\\nabla\\phi=Qe$ and tending to zero at infinity. Set $$F=h-\\phi\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathbb R^2\\setminus A,\n \\qquad p=e-\\nabla\\phi\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathbb R^2.$$ Then $p\\in L^2(\\mathbb R^2;\\mathbb R^2)$, $F\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus A)$ for every $R>0$, and $\\nabla F=p$ off $A$. Distributionally on the whole plane, $$\\operatorname{div}p=0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{supp}(\\operatorname{curl}p)\\subset A,\\qquad\n \\Delta p=\\nabla^\\perp\\operatorname{curl}p.\n \\label{eq:compact-field-equations}$$ Outside a sufficiently large disk, $F$ and $p$ are smooth, and $$F(x)=O(|x|^{-1}),\\qquad\n |\\nabla^k F(x)|=O_k(|x|^{-1-k})\\quad(k\\geq1).\n \\label{eq:compact-field-decay}$$ In particular $p(x)=O(|x|^{-2})$ there.",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $A\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be compact and have zero area. Distributionally on the whole plane, $$\\operatorname{div}p=0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{supp}(\\operatorname{curl}p)\\subset A,\\qquad\n \\Delta p=\\nabla^\\perp\\operatorname{curl}p.\n \\label{eq:compact-field-equations}$$ Outside a sufficiently large disk, $F$ and $p$ are smooth, and $$F(x)=O(|x|^{-1}),\\qquad\n |\\nabla^k F(x)|=O_k(|x|^{-1-k})\\quad(k\\geq1).\n \\label{eq:compact-field-decay}$$ In particular $p(x)=O(|x|^{-2})$ there.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-translated-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Then these integrals converge absolutely, and for every $|t|<\\delta$ and $\\sigma\\in\\mathbb R$, $$0\\leq 2\\bigl(I_p(t)-I_p(0)\\bigr)\n     +2\\sigma\\bigl(I_q(t)+\\langle p,q\\rangle_{L^2}\\bigr)\n     +\\sigma^2\\|q\\|_{L^2}^2.\n \\label{eq:translated-energy}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-366.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-translated-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H=A\\cup B\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be closed, rectifiable, and locally of finite $\\mathcal H^1$ measure, where $A$ is compact, $B$ is closed, and $\\operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0$. Let $(v,H)$ be an absolute minimizer of $E_0$ under compactly supported comparisons, with $$v\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus H)\\quad(R>0),\\qquad\n \\int_{B_R}|\\nabla v|^2\\leq C(1+R)\\quad(R\\geq1).$$ Suppose $v=f+F$ off $H$, where $f\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus B)$ for every $R>0$, and $(F,p)$ is obtained from a compactly supported $h\\in W^{1,2}(\\mathbb R^2\\setminus A)$ by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:compact-projection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:compact-projection\">7</a>. Put $G=\\nabla v-p$, and assume $$G=\\nabla f\\text{ off }B,\\qquad\n \\int_{B_R}|G|^2\\leq C'(1+R)\\quad(R\\geq1).$$ For any compactly supported $h'\\in W^{1,2}(\\mathbb R^2\\setminus A)$, let $(P,q)$ be its counterpart of $(F,p)$ in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:compact-projection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:compact-projection\">7</a>. Choose $\\delta>0$ so that $A_t\\cap B=\\varnothing$ whenever $|t|<\\delta$. Define $$I_p(t)=\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}G\\cdot p_t,\n \\qquad I_q(t)=\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}G\\cdot q_t.$$ Then these integrals converge absolutely, and for every $|t|<\\delta$ and $\\sigma\\in\\mathbb R$, $$0\\leq 2\\bigl(I_p(t)-I_p(0)\\bigr)\n     +2\\sigma\\bigl(I_q(t)+\\langle p,q\\rangle_{L^2}\\bigr)\n     +\\sigma^2\\|q\\|_{L^2}^2.\n \\label{eq:translated-energy}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $H=A\\cup B\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be closed, rectifiable, and locally of finite $\\mathcal H^1$ measure, where $A$ is compact, $B$ is closed, and $\\operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0$.       "explanation": "A distant cutoff turns translation and an independent jump perturbation into compactly supported competitors. Length and translated self-energy cancel, yielding an absolutely convergent interaction inequality without subtracting infinite whole-plane energies.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-harmonic-interaction",
        "exact_quote": "After decreasing $\\delta$ if necessary, $I_p$ is harmonic on $B_\\delta(0)$ as a function of the translation parameter. Consequently $$I_p(t)=I_p(0),\\qquad\n I_q(t)=-\\langle p,q\\rangle_{L^2}\n       \\quad(|t|<\\delta)\n \\label{eq:constant-interactions}$$ for every compactly supported $h'$ allowed in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:translated-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:translated-comparison\">8</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-366.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-harmonic-interaction",
          "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:translated-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:translated-comparison\">8</a>, suppose in addition that $f$ is harmonic on $O=\\mathbb R^2\\setminus B$.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-weak-l4",
        "exact_quote": "For every open $U\\Subset\\Omega$ there is a constant $C_U<\\infty$, depending on $U$ and the minimizer, such that $$\\label{eq:weak-l4}\n \\mathcal L^2\\bigl(\\{x\\in U\\setminus K:|\\nabla u(x)|>t\\}\\bigr)\n \\le C_Ut^{-4}\\qquad\\text{for every }t>0,$$ where $\\mathcal L^2$ denotes planar Lebesgue measure.         "source_id": "PM-366.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-weak-l4",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(u,K)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>. For every open $U\\Subset\\Omega$ there is a constant $C_U<\\infty$, depending on $U$ and the minimizer, such that $$\\label{eq:weak-l4}\n \\mathcal L^2\\bigl(\\{x\\in U\\setminus K:|\\nabla u(x)|>t\\}\\bigr)\n \\le C_Ut^{-4}\\qquad\\text{for every }t>0,$$ where $\\mathcal L^2$ denotes planar Lebesgue measure.       "exact_statement": "Let $(u,K)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.           "anchor": "stmt-cor-weak-l4",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(u,K)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-weak-l4",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\nabla u$ denote the approximate gradient furnished by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:initial-reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:initial-reduction\">2</a>; it agrees almost everywhere on $\\Omega\\setminus K$ with the gradient in <a href=\"#eq:original-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-energy\">[eq:original-energy]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-local-weak-gradient-integrability",
      "kind": "bound",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-weak-l4",
          "exact_quote": "For every open $U\\Subset\\Omega$ there is a constant $C_U<\\infty$, depending on $U$ and the minimizer, such that $$\\label{eq:weak-l4}\n \\mathcal L^2\\bigl(\\{x\\in U\\setminus K:|\\nabla u(x)|>t\\}\\bigr)\n \\le C_Ut^{-4}\\qquad\\text{for every }t>0,$$ where $\\mathcal L^2$ denotes planar Lebesgue measure.",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-weak-l4",
          "exact_quote": "In particular, $\\nabla u\\in L^{4,\\infty}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$ and $\\nabla u\\in L^p_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$ for every $0<p<4$.",
          "source_id": "PM-366.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-cor-weak-l4"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-366.1",

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Admissible pair** ([source](#eq:original-energy)): A pair $(u,K)$ is *admissible* if $$K\subset\Omega\text{ is relatively closed},\qquad \mathcal H^1(K)<\infty,\qquad u\in L^2(\Omega),\qquad u\|_{\Omega\setminus K}\in W^{1,2}(\Omega\setminus K).$$
- **Fidelity energy** ([source](#eq:original-energy)): For open $V\subset\Omega$, set $$\label{eq:original-energy} \mathcal F_g(u,K;V) =\int_{V\setminus K}\|\nabla u\|^2\,dx +\mathcal H^1(K\cap V)+\int_V\|u-g\|^2\,dx.$$
- **Absolute minimizer** ([source](#sec:problem)): An admissible pair is an *absolute minimizer* if, for every open $V\Subset\Omega$ and every admissible $(w,L)$ with $$\label{eq:compact-comparison} (L\mathbin{\triangle}K)\cup\operatorname{spt}(w-u)\Subset V,$$ one has $\mathcal F_g(u,K;V)\le\mathcal F_g(w,L;V)$.
- **Reduced pair** ([source](#sec:problem)): The pair is *reduced* if $$\label{eq:reduced} K=\operatorname{spt}_{\Omega}(\mathcal H^1\!\llcorner K).$$
- **Special bounded variation** ([source](#sec:initial-reduction)): The space $SBV(W)$ consists of the $BV$ functions whose Cantor part vanishes.
- **Smooth-data weak Dirichlet energy** ([source](#eq:weak-dirichlet-energy)): For a disk $D$ and $w\in C^1_c(\mathbb R^2)$, consider the functional $$\mathcal J_D(z) :=\int_D\bigl(\|\nabla z\|^2+\|z-g\|^2\bigr)\,dx +\mathcal H^1(J_z\cap\overline D) \label{eq:weak-dirichlet-energy}$$ on $z\in SBV(\mathbb R^2)$ satisfying $z=w$ almost everywhere on $\mathbb R^2\setminus D$.
- **Homogeneous energy** ([source](#sec:known-facts)): For a closed rectifiable set $H\subset\mathbb R^2$ of locally finite length and a function $v\in W^{1,2}(D\setminus H)$ for each bounded open $D$, define $$E_0(v,H;V)=\int_{V\setminus H}\|\nabla v\|^2\,dx +\mathcal H^1(H\cap V).$$
- **Generalized minimizer** ([source](#sec:known-facts)): Throughout this paper, *generalized minimizer* means a limit of *reduced absolute* minimizers in the sense of (De Lellis and Focardi 2025, Definition 2.2.4) [[delellis_focardi2025](#ref-delellis_focardi2025)]; a *global* generalized minimizer has limiting domain $\mathbb R^2$.
- **Whole-plane gradient projection** ([source](#stmt-lem-compact-projection)): Let $$Z=\overline{\{\nabla\zeta:\zeta\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2)\}} ^{\,L^2(\mathbb R^2;\mathbb R^2)},$$ and let $Q$ be the orthogonal projection onto $Z$.
- **Global edges** ([source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): Define the edges to be the connected components left after deleting these endpoints and triple junctions from $K$.

| q-length-density-lower | \mathcal H^1(H\cap B_r(y)) | ≥ | $c r$ | Centers on the crack and positive radii; the dimensional constant is not numerically specified. |
| q-length-density-upper | \mathcal H^1(H\cap B_r(y)) | ≤ | $C r$ | Centers on the crack and positive radii; the dimensional constant is not numerically specified. |
| q-epsilon-regularity-scale | s | ≤ | $1$ | The local model scale; the closeness threshold depends on the fixed fidelity bounds. |
| q-exterior-compact-field-gradient-decay | p(x) | = | $O(\|x\|^{-2})$ | Exterior spatial decay; constants depend on the input field. |
| q-local-gradient-superlevel-area | \mathcal L^2\bigl(\{x\in U\setminus K:\|\nabla u(x)\|>t\}\bigr) | ≤ | $C_Ut^{-4}$ | All positive superlevel thresholds; the constant depends on the region and the minimizer. |
| q-strong-gradient-integrability-exponent | p | < | $4$ | Positive exponents strictly below the weak endpoint. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-dirichlet-input** [claim](#stmt-lem-dirichlet-input) / [proof](#eq:dirichlet-essential-closure): Use the cited smooth-data existence and essential-closure theorem, then boundary density and differentiation of measures to exclude additional closure length on the circle. -> A bounded auxiliary minimizer with compact finite-length closed jump set and matching traces on jump-free boundary portions.
- **step-reduce-original-pair** [claim](#stmt-prop-initial-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dirichlet-input): Obtain good-circle traces, clip to prove local boundedness, use finite-ball extensions to obtain special bounded variation, and compare smooth-data Dirichlet replacements while sending the boundary error to zero. -> Local boundedness, rectifiability, equality of crack and jump lengths, and reduced restrictions on smooth interior regions.
- **step-planar-theory** [claim](#stmt-item-epsilon) / [proof](#sec:known-facts): Apply the cited compactness and regularity results to reduced rectifiable restrictions, retaining relative constants. Connectedness of the limiting complement makes the exterior separation constraint vacuous. -> Global generalized limits, growth and density bounds, connected-complement absolute comparison, conditional classification, and epsilon regularity.
- **step-isolate-compact-piece** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-isolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-isolation): Represent a compact-space component as the intersection of clopen neighborhoods and use a finite boundary cover to isolate a relatively open compact piece. Density of the complement preserves connectedness after removal. -> A positive-length compact piece at positive distance from a closed remainder.
- **step-project-compact-jump** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-projection): Project onto the closed space of whole-plane gradients, normalize the potential using its exterior harmonic expansion, and identify the divergence-free remainder and its curl support. -> A finite-energy jump field with exterior decay and a uniquely normalized projected potential.
- **step-translate-and-cut-off** [claim](#stmt-lem-translated-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-translated-comparison): Translate the compact set and its fields, add an independent jump perturbation, and glue back to the original function on a distant annulus. Estimate the annular error and interactions before passing to the limit. -> The finite-translation quadratic energy inequality with absolutely convergent interactions.
- **step-interaction-constancy** [claim](#stmt-lem-harmonic-interaction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-harmonic-interaction): Split the interaction near and away from the translated compact set, establish harmonicity in the translation parameter, apply the minimum principle, and eliminate the linear coefficient of the nonnegative value-variation polynomial. -> Constant translation interaction and the identity for every independent compact jump variation.
- **step-exclude-positive-length-piece** [claim](#stmt-prop-compact-piece) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-harmonic-interaction-2): Construct the harmonic background, remove the projected potential by distributional testing and decay, and prescribe arbitrary one-sided jumps on a regular arc. Translation constancy forces an affine background; energy growth and the interaction identity annihilate both gradient fields, making the compact piece removable. -> A strict length-saving comparison contradicting minimality.
- **step-classify-global-limits** [claim](#stmt-cor-classification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-classification): Use the known classification for disconnected complements. For a connected complement, isolate any bounded component and exclude the resulting compact piece using almost-everywhere arc neighborhoods; then apply the remaining known classification. -> The complete closed-set classification for the specified generalized limits.
- **step-transfer-models-and-count-components** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-classification-2): Choose a local model in a classified nonempty blow-up, transfer bilateral closeness to the original scale, and apply epsilon regularity. Recenter at the actual endpoint or junction when necessary. Cover the compact interior crack by finitely many connected model neighborhoods. -> Interior local models and finiteness of global crack components meeting each relatively compact open region.
- **step-derive-weak-gradient-bound** [claim](#stmt-cor-weak-l4) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-weak-l4): Restrict to a smooth interior region, identify the local models with the cited regular strata, and apply the equivalence between absence of irregular points and the weak gradient estimate. Use the layer-cake formula for strong integrability below the endpoint. -> Local weak endpoint gradient control and strong integrability for the stated smaller exponents.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-interior-crack-geometry**: Every interior crack point has a neighborhood consisting of an embedded regular arc, an arc ending at that point, or three regular arcs meeting only at that point with pairwise angles of 120 degrees.
- **invariant-global-component-local-finiteness**: Every relatively compact open region meets only finitely many global connected components of the closed discontinuity set.
- **invariant-generalized-limit-classification**: The closed set of a global generalized minimizer arising from absolute minimizers is empty, a line, a half-line, or a triod of half-lines meeting at 120 degrees.
- **invariant-separated-compact-piece-exclusion**: A homogeneous absolute minimizer with connected complement and the stated energy growth cannot contain a separated compact piece of positive length containing an open regular subarc.
- **invariant-harmonic-interaction-constancy**: The interaction of the translated compact-jump field with the harmonic background is constant on a sufficiently small translation disk; independent jump variations satisfy the associated interaction identity.
- **invariant-local-weak-gradient-bound**: On every relatively compact open region, the gradient superlevel area is bounded by a minimizer-dependent constant times the inverse fourth power of the threshold. The approximate gradient belongs locally to weak fourth-power integrability and to strong integrability for every positive exponent below four.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-boundary-extension**: Extend the local component count or crack geometry to the domain boundary. -> The asserted finiteness and regularity concern interior neighborhoods and compactly contained observation regions.
- **anti-use-truncated-component-count**: Count connected components of the intersection with an arbitrary observation region using the global component theorem. -> A single global component can produce infinitely many components after intersection with an arbitrary relatively compact open set.
- **anti-use-stationarity-as-minimality**: Apply the geometry theorem to a stationary reconstruction or an unverified numerical minimizer. -> First variations alone do not establish the comparison inequality for changes of both the function and the discontinuity set.
- **anti-use-unreduced-closed-additions**: Apply the geometric classification to a closed set with arbitrary zero-length additions. -> Such additions do not affect the energy but need not obey the asserted local crack geometry.
- **anti-use-unbounded-or-nonplanar-data**: Transfer the theorem to unbounded fidelity data or a nonplanar segmentation problem. -> The theorem's domain and fidelity hypotheses do not cover either extension.
- **anti-use-strong-endpoint-integrability**: Replace the weak gradient endpoint estimate with strong fourth-power integrability near every crack tip. -> The crack-tip profile has a gradient whose fourth power is not locally integrable.
- **anti-use-infinite-energy-subtraction**: Justify the translated comparison by subtracting whole-plane Dirichlet energies directly. -> The background is controlled by local growth rather than global finite energy, so the formal subtraction need not be defined.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results concern continuum absolute minimizers, Sobolev functions, Hausdorff limits, and harmonic unique continuation. They do not supply a finite constructive algorithm for finding or certifying a minimizer. The comparison definition requires every admissible compact change, and the compact-piece proof uses the source statement: "Unique continuation for harmonic functions makes $f$ affine on the connected open set $O$." Finite numerical examples cannot decide those hypotheses or certify the universal interior regularity and generalized-limit classification.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The interior geometry can inform specifications for segmentation software, but the paper supplies neither an executable segmentation procedure nor a certificate that a computed reconstruction is an absolute minimizer. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The crack-tip gradient estimate offers a qualitative reference for planar scalar simulations. It provides no time evolution, fracture propagation rule, or finite simulation construction. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The distinction between global crack components and components of a truncated set can guide geometric inspection tools. The paper does not construct a finite component-counting procedure for sampled curves. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact-piece isolation argument uses clopen neighborhoods and compactness to establish existence. It does not specify a finite input representation or an algorithm for finding the isolated piece. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The homogeneous energy is defined on closed rectifiable sets and Sobolev functions. These continuum objects are not accompanied by a discrete representation, storage invariant, or update construction. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The gradient projection is an orthogonal projection in a whole-plane function space, with a normalization at infinity. No finite computational kernel, arithmetic specification, or hardware mapping is constructed. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Harmonic interaction constancy follows from continuum minimality and the harmonic minimum principle. It establishes no security property, adversarial model, or cryptographic construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The functional's image segmentation origin suggests a geometric prior for reconstruction models. The paper contains no training construction and does not establish that learned boundaries satisfy absolute minimality. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The translated comparison supplies a variational necessary inequality for a continuum absolute minimizer while preserving crack length. It provides no finite optimization method or convergence contract for numerical iterates. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The global classification concerns connected components of planar discontinuity sets. It supplies no distributed computation, communication protocol, or consistency construction. |
| other (Continuum image segmentation analysis) | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The admissible scalar reconstruction and finite-length discontinuity set define a continuum segmentation model. The regularity results support analysis of exact minimizers, but do not construct a finite segmentation implementation. |

          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-compact-isolation</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Let $C$ be the given component. It is compact, so choose $R$ with $C\\subset B_R$ and set $X=H\\cap\\overline{B_R}$. The component in $X$ of any $p\\in C$ is exactly $C$.\n\nWe use the compact-space fact that a component is the intersection of all its clopen neighborhoods. For completeness, in a compact metric space let $Q_n$ be the points joined to $p$ by a finite chain with consecutive distances less than $1/n$. Each $Q_n$ is clopen. Their nested intersection $Q$ is connected: otherwise the two compact pieces of a separation admit disjoint neighborhoods at positive distance; for large $n$, compactness puts $Q_n$ in their union, where a $1/n$-chain cannot join the two pieces. Thus $Q$ is the component of $p$, proving the stated fact.\n\nFor each $z\\in X\\cap\\partial B_R$, choose a clopen neighborhood of $C$ in $X$ which omits $z$. Finitely many of their complements cover $X\\cap\\partial B_R$; take $A$ to be the intersection of the corresponding neighborhoods. If $X\\cap\\partial B_R$ is empty, take $A=X$. In either case $A$ is compact, contains $C$, and is disjoint from $\\partial B_R$. Its positive distance from that circle shows that its relative openness in $X$ also gives relative openness in $H$. Consequently $B=H\\setminus A$ is closed, and its distance from compact $A$ is positive. Local finite length gives $\\mathcal H^1(A)<\\infty$; the support assumption and relative openness give $\\mathcal H^1(A)>0$.\n\nFinally, $D=\\mathbb R^2\\setminus H$ is dense because $H$ has locally finite length. A set lying between a connected set and its closure is connected. The larger complement $\\mathbb R^2\\setminus B$ lies between $D$ and $\\overline D=\\mathbb R^2$, which proves the last assertion. ◻",
          "source_object": "Compact-piece isolation proof"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-planar-bounded-fidelity-setting** (hypothesis): The interior regularity theorem requires a bounded planar Lipschitz domain and bounded fidelity datum. Its conclusions do not establish corresponding regularity in other dimensions or for unbounded data. Blocked use: ['Apply the local arc and junction classification to volumetric discontinuity sets.', 'Claim the same regularity for an unbounded fidelity datum.']
- **limit-absolute-comparison-versus-stationarity** (hypothesis): The minimizing hypothesis concerns admissible compact changes of both the function and the discontinuity set. Value-only stationarity or numerical agreement with the local models does not establish this hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Certify a stationary reconstruction by checking its Euler equation alone.', 'Transfer the theorem to an approximate segmentation output without establishing absolute comparison.']
- **limit-reducedness-and-admissible-length** (hypothesis): The geometric theorem applies to the support of the crack-length measure. Admissibility requires finite crack length and the stated function-space membership; arbitrary closed zero-length additions are removed by reducedness. Blocked use: ['Apply the local geometry theorem to an unreduced crack carrying arbitrary zero-length additions.', 'Treat a closed set without finite length or the stated Sobolev function as an admissible minimizing pair.']
- **limit-interior-scope-and-global-components** (formal_scope): Component and edge finiteness are interior assertions about global connected pieces. They neither establish boundary finiteness nor bound the number of components created by intersecting the crack with an arbitrary observation region. Blocked use: ['Infer component finiteness up to the domain boundary.', 'Replace global component identity with connectivity inside a cropped region.']
- **limit-dirichlet-replacement-and-boundary-errors** (hypothesis): The auxiliary Dirichlet input uses a disk, bounded fidelity data, and smooth compactly supported exterior data. The reduction accounts separately for mismatched weak traces and the extra boundary arcs inserted into the closed comparison set. Blocked use: ['Invoke the auxiliary replacement theorem for arbitrary exterior data without an additional result.', 'Discard boundary mismatch length when comparing the replacement with the original pair.', 'Assume the smooth boundary approximations have uniformly bounded derivatives.']
- **limit-specified-generalized-limit-class** (formal_scope): The classification concerns generalized limits of reduced absolute minimizers, retaining relative additive constants between complementary components. Componentwise harmonic limits alone do not supply the full limiting object or the required Sobolev control near the closed set. Blocked use: ['Apply the classification to an arbitrary harmonic function paired with a closed crack.', 'Discard relative constants between complementary components.', 'Infer square integrability up to the crack solely from convergence away from it.']
- **limit-connected-complement-comparison** (hypothesis): Connectedness of the limiting complement removes the exterior separation restriction and permits absolute compact comparisons whose support meets the crack. That reduction cannot be assumed when the complement is disconnected. Blocked use: ['Use the compact-piece exclusion argument for a disconnected complement without checking the comparison class.', 'Require jump variations to vanish on the crack and then claim the same rigidity argument.']
- **limit-isolation-requires-support-and-separation** (hypothesis): Compact isolation uses local finite length and the support condition. The resulting compact piece may contain several components; a bounded component itself need not already be separated from the rest of the closed set. Blocked use: ['Translate a bounded component without first establishing positive separation from a closed remainder.', 'Assume the isolated compact piece must be connected.', 'Infer positive length of the isolated piece without the support hypothesis.']
- **limit-whole-plane-projection-and-curl-support** (formal_scope): The projection uses a compact zero-area set and a compactly supported off-set Sobolev function. Its potential is normalized at infinity. The curl of the remainder field is supported on the compact set; the field itself need not have compact support. Blocked use: ['Replace the whole-plane projection with a bounded-domain projection while assuming identical decay and interaction identities.', 'Interpret curl support as support of the remainder field.', 'Apply the projection lemma to a positive-area excluded set or an input without the stated compact support.']
- **limit-translation-growth-and-cutoff** (hypothesis): The translated comparison requires positive separation, the projected decomposition, local Sobolev membership, and the stated growth bounds. Exterior decay and a distant cutoff justify the limiting interaction inequality; direct subtraction of infinite whole-plane energies does not. Blocked use: ['Translate the compact piece through the fixed remainder.', 'Subtract whole-plane Dirichlet energies without first proving the finite comparison and interaction convergence.', 'Assume the background field has globally finite energy merely from its local growth bound.']
- **limit-harmonic-background-and-independent-jumps** (hypothesis): Interaction constancy requires a background harmonic across the compact piece. The subsequent rigidity argument uses independently prescribed one-sided jump traces on a neighborhood where the entire closed set is a regular arc, together with connectedness and energy growth. Blocked use: ['Use the harmonic minimum principle for a background not known to be harmonic.', 'Infer affine rigidity from a single fixed jump perturbation.', 'Apply the compact-piece exclusion to an arc embedded in an otherwise uncontrolled local crack configuration.']
- **limit-epsilon-regularity-scale-and-center** (hypothesis): Model transfer requires bilateral Hausdorff closeness on the enlarged ball, the prescribed scale restriction, and fixed bounds on fidelity data. The endpoint or junction furnished by the resulting chart need not coincide with the original center. Blocked use: ['Infer full local regularity from unilateral proximity of a sampled crack to a model.', 'Identify the center of an approximate model with the actual endpoint or junction.', 'Treat the closeness threshold as a supplied numerical tolerance.']
- **limit-weak-endpoint-and-dependent-constant** (edge_case): The endpoint gradient estimate is weak integrability with a constant depending on the interior region and the minimizer. Strong integrability is stated only below the endpoint; the crack-tip discussion explains that the endpoint power need not be locally integrable. Blocked use: ['Replace the weak endpoint estimate with strong endpoint integrability.', 'Use a universal numerical superlevel constant independent of the minimizer.', 'Extend the interior gradient bound to the domain boundary.']
- **limit-no-finite-construction-or-count-bound** (nonconstructive): The proof uses continuum minimizer existence, subsequence compactness, infinite-dimensional projection, harmonic unique continuation, and a compact-cover argument. It supplies no finite solver, effective neighborhood extraction procedure, or explicit component-count bound. Blocked use: ['Advertise a certified finite algorithm for constructing an absolute minimizer from these results.', 'Treat finite numerical examples as certificates of the universal classification.', 'Convert qualitative local finiteness into an explicit component-count budget.']

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "item:blowup",
          "quote": "Let $(u,K)$ be a reduced, rectifiable absolute minimizer of the fidelity functional in an open set $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^2$, with bounded fidelity datum and locally bounded $u$. For each $x\\in K$ and each sequence $r_j\\downarrow0$, a subsequence of $$K_j=\\frac{K-x}{r_j},\\qquad\n     u_j(y)=\\frac{u(x+r_jy)}{\\sqrt{r_j}}$$ on the expanding domains $(\\Omega-x)/r_j$ has a global generalized limit $(v,H,\\{p_{kl}\\})$. The sets converge locally in the bilateral Hausdorff sense. The set $H$ is closed, rectifiable, locally of finite length, and is the support of $\\mathcal H^1\\!\\llcorner H$; moreover $0\\in H$. For every bounded open $D\\Subset\\mathbb R^2$, $v\\in W^{1,2}(D\\setminus H)$.",
          "quote_sha256": "b5f73fa2255f697065830c8fc26cda0bb85bf0013c2f656be2cb3ecbb2dc078a"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-classification-2",
          "quote": "If $U\\Subset\\Omega$ is open, then $K\\cap\\overline U$ is compact, so finitely many of these disks cover it.",
          "quote_sha256": "f25ff86b016ea2c17ed5c39f1a9cb371dc547016bbf7993e4085586875d2e378"
        }
      ],

  "prerequisite_chains": [

      "concept": "Interior model classification",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-6",
          "quote": "#### Proof strategy\n\nWe first pass from the stated finite-length formulation to a locally bounded, rectifiable minimizing pair. The reduction uses clipping inside disks and a Dirichlet comparison with smooth approximations of the boundary trace; it preserves the original pair.\n\nAt an interior point $x$, the rescaled closed sets $(K-x)/r_j$, with $r_j\\downarrow0$, have global generalized limits $H$. These are limits of absolute minimizers, with additive constants in each complementary component recorded as part of the limiting data. The established compactness and rigidity theorems reduce their classification to excluding a bounded component when $\\mathbb R^2\\setminus H$ is connected. In this case the limit function $v$ is an absolute minimizer of the zero-fidelity energy on the whole plane.\n\nA bounded component can be enlarged to a compact piece $A\\subset H$ at positive distance from $B=H\\setminus A$. We split the gradient of $v$ into a field carrying the jumps across $A$ and the gradient of a harmonic potential that extends through $A$. Translating $A$ leaves its length unchanged. The interaction between these two fields is harmonic in the translation parameter; minimality forces it to be constant. Independent changes in the jump values along a regular subarc of $A$ then force this harmonic potential to be affine. The energy growth bound makes its gradient zero; the value-variation identity then makes the remaining field zero. Removing $A$ saves positive length, a contradiction.\n\nThe known classification now gives only a line, a halfline or three rays for a nonempty blow-up set. Epsilon regularity transfers these models to the original minimizer. Finally, a finite cover of $K\\cap\\overline U$ by connected model neighborhoods proves <a href=\"#eq:component-finiteness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:component-finiteness\">[eq:component-finiteness]</a>. The resulting absence of irregular points also yields the local weak-$L^4$ gradient estimate through the De Lellis–Focardi equivalence.\n\nThe reduction occupies Section <a href=\"#sec:initial-reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:initial-reduction\">2</a>, and Section <a href=\"#sec:known-facts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:known-facts\">3</a> records the compactness and regularity results used below. Sections <a href=\"#sec:isolation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:isolation\">4</a> and <a href=\"#sec:compact-piece\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:compact-piece\">5</a> establish the compact-piece exclusion. Section <a href=\"#sec:assembly\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:assembly\">6</a> completes the classification, proves Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, and derives the weak-$L^4$ consequence.",
          "quote_sha256": "a0dbd5baad8d48fdd1cccca4b8235ce4a9c8d74111fcb7af21ef27f34b8e9e20"
        }
      ],
      "requires_concepts": [

    {
      "concept": "Harmonic interaction rigidity",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-projection",
          "quote": "Let $A\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be compact and have zero area. Suppose $h\\in W^{1,2}(\\mathbb R^2\\setminus A)$ has compact support, and write $e=\\nabla h$ for its gradient density. Let $$Z=\\overline{\\{\\nabla\\zeta:\\zeta\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^2)\\}}\n       ^{\\,L^2(\\mathbb R^2;\\mathbb R^2)},$$ and let $Q$ be the orthogonal projection onto $Z$. There is a unique $\\phi\\in W^{1,2}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbb R^2)$ satisfying $\\nabla\\phi=Qe$ and tending to zero at infinity. Set $$F=h-\\phi\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathbb R^2\\setminus A,\n \\qquad p=e-\\nabla\\phi\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathbb R^2.$$ Then $p\\in L^2(\\mathbb R^2;\\mathbb R^2)$, $F\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus A)$ for every $R>0$, and $\\nabla F=p$ off $A$. Distributionally on the whole plane, $$\\operatorname{div}p=0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{supp}(\\operatorname{curl}p)\\subset A,\\qquad\n \\Delta p=\\nabla^\\perp\\operatorname{curl}p.\n \\label{eq:compact-field-equations}$$ Outside a sufficiently large disk, $F$ and $p$ are smooth, and $$F(x)=O(|x|^{-1}),\\qquad\n |\\nabla^k F(x)|=O_k(|x|^{-1-k})\\quad(k\\geq1).\n \\label{eq:compact-field-decay}$$ In particular $p(x)=O(|x|^{-2})$ there.",
          "quote_sha256": "8138a672142a404cf541fb9c5d3f0839920b912344f80c423d3c51209b84dce6"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-translated-comparison",
          "quote": "Let $H=A\\cup B\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be closed, rectifiable, and locally of finite $\\mathcal H^1$ measure, where $A$ is compact, $B$ is closed, and $\\operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0$. Let $(v,H)$ be an absolute minimizer of $E_0$ under compactly supported comparisons, with $$v\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus H)\\quad(R>0),\\qquad\n \\int_{B_R}|\\nabla v|^2\\leq C(1+R)\\quad(R\\geq1).$$ Suppose $v=f+F$ off $H$, where $f\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus B)$ for every $R>0$, and $(F,p)$ is obtained from a compactly supported $h\\in W^{1,2}(\\mathbb R^2\\setminus A)$ by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:compact-projection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:compact-projection\">7</a>. Put $G=\\nabla v-p$, and assume $$G=\\nabla f\\text{ off }B,\\qquad\n \\int_{B_R}|G|^2\\leq C'(1+R)\\quad(R\\geq1).$$ For any compactly supported $h'\\in W^{1,2}(\\mathbb R^2\\setminus A)$, let $(P,q)$ be its counterpart of $(F,p)$ in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:compact-projection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:compact-projection\">7</a>. Choose $\\delta>0$ so that $A_t\\cap B=\\varnothing$ whenever $|t|<\\delta$. Define $$I_p(t)=\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}G\\cdot p_t,\n \\qquad I_q(t)=\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}G\\cdot q_t.$$ Then these integrals converge absolutely, and for every $|t|<\\delta$ and $\\sigma\\in\\mathbb R$, $$0\\leq 2\\bigl(I_p(t)-I_p(0)\\bigr)\n     +2\\sigma\\bigl(I_q(t)+\\langle p,q\\rangle_{L^2}\\bigr)\n     +\\sigma^2\\|q\\|_{L^2}^2.\n \\label{eq:translated-energy}$$",
          "quote_sha256": "cc94d972508cf1ba2be881a667d2059c384dd717fd65be638617d655e611dea4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-harmonic-interaction",
          "quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:translated-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:translated-comparison\">8</a>, suppose in addition that $f$ is harmonic on $O=\\mathbb R^2\\setminus B$. After decreasing $\\delta$ if necessary, $I_p$ is harmonic on $B_\\delta(0)$ as a function of the translation parameter. Consequently $$I_p(t)=I_p(0),\\qquad\n I_q(t)=-\\langle p,q\\rangle_{L^2}\n       \\quad(|t|<\\delta)\n \\label{eq:constant-interactions}$$ for every compactly supported $h'$ allowed in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:translated-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:translated-comparison\">8</a>.",
          "quote_sha256": "17f82232c7c7020215aef1c2848149d6c642f349d98fad143f728dac0d77c80a"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-harmonic-interaction-2",
          "quote": "Unique continuation for harmonic functions makes $f$ affine on the connected open set $O$.",
          "quote_sha256": "69f028197adf96136c4f9dbe9bc862e117d86c1eb6c6f64c03eb5aaac7d58673"
        }
      ],

      "answer_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "quote": "Let $(u,K)$ be a reduced absolute minimizer of <a href=\"#eq:original-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-energy\">[eq:original-energy]</a> in a bounded Lipschitz domain $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^2$, with $g\\in L^\\infty(\\Omega)$. Every $x\\in K$ has an interior neighborhood in which $K$ has one of the following forms, with $C^{1,\\alpha}$ arcs for some $\\alpha>0$:*\n\n1.  *a single embedded arc with $x$ in its interior;*\n\n2.  *a single embedded arc ending at $x$;*\n\n3.  *three embedded arcs meeting only at $x$, with pairwise angles $120$ degrees.*\n\n\n*Moreover, for every open $U\\Subset\\Omega$, $$\\label{eq:component-finiteness}\n \\#\\{C\\in\\pi_0(K):C\\cap U\\ne\\varnothing\\}<\\infty,$$ where $\\pi_0(K)$ denotes the set of global connected components of $K$.",
          "quote_sha256": "93ff7ee7ede01db64410ee66a3d19730bdf7c246f6908155d81b4df4f87ea08b"
        }
      ],

    },
    {
      "answer_anchor": "eq:component-finiteness",
      "basis": "stated",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "quote": "Let $(u,K)$ be a reduced absolute minimizer of <a href=\"#eq:original-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-energy\">[eq:original-energy]</a> in a bounded Lipschitz domain $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^2$, with $g\\in L^\\infty(\\Omega)$. Every $x\\in K$ has an interior neighborhood in which $K$ has one of the following forms, with $C^{1,\\alpha}$ arcs for some $\\alpha>0$:*\n\n1.  *a single embedded arc with $x$ in its interior;*\n\n2.  *a single embedded arc ending at $x$;*\n\n3.  *three embedded arcs meeting only at $x$, with pairwise angles $120$ degrees.*\n\n\n*Moreover, for every open $U\\Subset\\Omega$, $$\\label{eq:component-finiteness}\n \\#\\{C\\in\\pi_0(K):C\\cap U\\ne\\varnothing\\}<\\infty,$$ where $\\pi_0(K)$ denotes the set of global connected components of $K$.",
          "quote_sha256": "93ff7ee7ede01db64410ee66a3d19730bdf7c246f6908155d81b4df4f87ea08b"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
          "quote": "The local models also make the endpoints and triple junctions a relatively closed discrete subset of $K$, so only finitely many of them meet a compact subset of $\\Omega$. Define the edges to be the connected components left after deleting these endpoints and triple junctions from $K$. In each model chart, deleting those points leaves at most three connected pieces, each contained in one global edge. A finite model-chart cover therefore meets only finitely many global edges. These are interior finiteness statements, with no assertion at $\\partial\\Omega$. They concern global components rather than components of $K\\cap U$: even one straight line can have infinitely many intersection components with an arbitrary relatively compact open set.",
          "quote_sha256": "4bf0277a5251adbaac14e8bd051c775c50a3c04570032701a354da124d13db95"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "The hypotheses of the main theorem and a relatively compact open region inside the domain; the count concerns global components of the crack.",
      "question": "Can global crack components accumulate infinitely in a relatively compact interior open region?",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "answer_anchor": "stmt-prop-initial-reduction",
      "basis": "stated",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-initial-reduction",
          "quote": "Let $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be bounded and open, let $g\\in L^\\infty(\\Omega)$, and let $(u,K)$ be an admissible absolute minimizer for $\\mathcal F_g$ as defined in Section <a href=\"#sec:problem\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:problem\">1.1</a>. In particular, $K$ is relatively closed, $\\mathcal H^1(K)<\\infty$, and $u\\in L^2(\\Omega)\\cap W^{1,2}(\\Omega\\setminus K)$; no rectifiability of $K$ is assumed. Then $$u\\in L^\\infty_{\\rm loc}(\\Omega)\\cap SBV_{\\rm loc}(\\Omega),\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal H^1(K\\setminus J_u)=0.$$ The function satisfies $\\Delta u=u-g$ on $\\Omega\\setminus K$ and has there a $C^{1,\\alpha}_{\\rm loc}$ representative for every $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$. If the pair is reduced, then $$K=\\operatorname{spt}_{\\Omega}(\\mathcal H^1\\!\\llcorner J_u)\n   =\\overline{J_u}^{\\,\\Omega}.\n \\label{eq:jump-support}$$ Consequently, in the reduced case, on every smooth open set $W\\Subset\\Omega$, the restricted pair is a reduced rectifiable absolute minimizer with bounded function.",
          "quote_sha256": "215a05f0d09c8aae8471f2eb857d200361f9b98281da5e7d93e0390e6975b50b"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "An admissible absolute minimizer with bounded fidelity datum in a bounded open planar domain; reducedness is needed for the support identification.",
      "question": "Does a finite-length admissible crack need to be assumed rectifiable before applying planar regularity theory?",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-compact-piece",
          "quote": "Let $H\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be closed, rectifiable, and locally of finite $\\mathcal H^1$ measure, and assume $D=\\mathbb R^2\\setminus H$ is connected. Let $(v,H)$ be an absolute minimizer of $E_0$ under compactly supported comparisons, with $$v\\in W^{1,2}(B_R\\setminus H)\\quad(R>0),\\qquad\n \\int_{B_R}|\\nabla v|^2\\leq C(1+R)\\quad(R\\geq1).$$ There is no compact set $A\\subset H$ satisfying all of the following:*\n\n1.  *$\\mathcal H^1(A)>0$;*\n\n2.  *$B=H\\setminus A$ is closed and $\\operatorname{dist}(A,B)>0$, with the distance interpreted as $+\\infty$ if $B=\\varnothing$;*\n\n3.  *$A$ contains an open subarc of $H$ that is an embedded $C^1$ arc.*\n\n*In the third condition, an open subarc means that some open neighborhood meets $H$ in just that arc.",

Consequently $$I_p(t)=I_p(0),\\qquad\n I_q(t)=-\\langle p,q\\rangle_{L^2}\n       \\quad(|t|<\\delta)\n \\label{eq:constant-interactions}$$ for every compactly supported $h'$ allowed in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:translated-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:translated-comparison\">8</a>.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-classification",
          "quote": "The closed set of every global generalized minimizer arising from absolute minimizers is empty, a line, a $120$-degree triod of half-lines, or a half-line.",
          "quote_sha256": "ca291d0aad0e20ee6887a4b4e139eed9597233c01ef57a82d94055695644609e"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "item:classification",
          "quote": "A global generalized minimizer has at most one unbounded connected component of its closed set. If its closed set disconnects the plane, that set is a line or three half-lines meeting at $120$ degrees. If all but at most one connected component of its closed set lie in one compact set, the set is empty, a line, three half-lines meeting at $120$ degrees, or a half-line.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-weak-l4",
          "quote": "Let $(u,K)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>. Let $\\nabla u$ denote the approximate gradient furnished by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:initial-reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:initial-reduction\">2</a>; it agrees almost everywhere on $\\Omega\\setminus K$ with the gradient in <a href=\"#eq:original-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:original-energy\">[eq:original-energy]</a>. For every open $U\\Subset\\Omega$ there is a constant $C_U<\\infty$, depending on $U$ and the minimizer, such that $$\\label{eq:weak-l4}\n \\mathcal L^2\\bigl(\\{x\\in U\\setminus K:|\\nabla u(x)|>t\\}\\bigr)\n \\le C_Ut^{-4}\\qquad\\text{for every }t>0,$$ where $\\mathcal L^2$ denotes planar Lebesgue measure. In particular, $\\nabla u\\in L^{4,\\infty}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$ and $\\nabla u\\in L^p_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$ for every $0<p<4$.",
          "quote_sha256": "77ade23737e95ecef4ba63a1021471d7c1a5c226696bab041990a6b65a88962f"
        }
      ],
